<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Deciding joint-update <i>k</i>-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence is $`\mathsf{EXPTIME}`$-complete when the two graphs are given by explicit adjacency matrices and <i>k</i> ≥ 2 is encoded in binary. The result holds even for connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Both $\textnormal{\textsc{WL-Equiv}}$ and $\textnormal{\textsc{Subcubic-WL-Equiv}}$ are $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$-complete under deterministic polynomial-time many-one reductions.
  - Explanation: Deciding equivalence at a supplied dimension is complete for exponential time, including the connected subcubic restriction.
- **C03** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dimension-cap)): Let $G,H$ have the same positive order $n$. For every $k\geq n$ with $k\geq 2$, the relation $G\equiv_k H$ holds if and only if $G$ and $H$ are isomorphic. Consequently, when $n\geq 2$ and $k\geq 2$, $$G\equiv_k H \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad G\equiv_{\min\{k,n\}}H.$$
  - Explanation: At dimensions at least the graph order, equivalence agrees with isomorphism; the supplied dimension can be capped before tuple allocation.
- **C04** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-membership)): Both $\textnormal{\textsc{WL-Equiv}}$ and $\textnormal{\textsc{Subcubic-WL-Equiv}}$ belong to $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$. More precisely, after checking their respective input conditions, an input of common graph order $n\ge2$ and total bit length $L$ can be decided in time $2^{O(n\log n)}L^{O(1)}$, with absolute constants in the exponents.
  - Explanation: Input validation and common tuple refinement give a uniform exponential-time decision procedure.
- **C05** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-wl-game)): For graphs $G,H$ of the same positive order and every $k\geq 2$, $G\equiv_k H$ if and only if Duplicator wins the game with $k+1$ pair slots.
  - Explanation: Joint tuple refinement is characterized by the announced-bijection game with the extra pair slot.
- **C07** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-computation)): Fix a deterministic one-tape machine $M$ with the initialization and frozen-halting convention just specified, and an integer polynomial $p$ satisfying $p(m)\ge m+2$ and bounding its running time by $2^{p(m)}$. For every word $w$ of length $m$, one can generate a product description in the sense of [def:computation-product](#def:computation-product) of a finite acyclic circuit at address length $r=2(p(m)+2)$, with scalar terminal tests, such that at least one test is true if and only if $M$ accepts $w$.* *There are $O_M(1)$ gate types and $O_M((r+1)^2+m)$ connection schemas and seed or test requests, each with $r$ constant-size coordinate entries. All domains have nonempty binary factors and all coordinate maps are injections. The total number of coordinate entries is $O_M(r((r+1)^2+m))$. The description is generated deterministically in time polynomial in $r+m$, with constants depending only on $M,p$, without enumerating times or full addresses.
  - Explanation: Carry and borrow schemas compile a fixed exponential-time machine into a polynomial-size product description without enumerating full addresses.
- **C09** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-consistency)): For every finite acyclic circuit with the conventions of Section <a href="#sec:computation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:computation">3</a>, one can construct nonempty scalar-image constraints with vector dimensions at most two which are consistent if and only if every test is false.* *If the circuit has a product description with $g$ gate types, $c$ connection schemas, and $b$ seed and test requests in total, the result is an instance of Definition <a href="#def:scalar-images" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:scalar-images">8</a> with $g+c+1$ real types and $3c+b$ comparison schemas, at the same address length. Its one-coordinate domains and maps are copied from that description or are identities. In particular, the circuit in Proposition <a href="#prop:computation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:computation">7</a> gives $O_M((r+1)^2+m)$ real types and schemas with dimensions in $\{1,2\}$.
  - Explanation: Private vector buffers translate directed circuit propagation into individual scalar-image equalities with the required false-test polarity.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-computation-images)): For every binary language $L_0\in\mathsf{EXPTIME}$, there are a fixed machine $M$ and fixed integer polynomial $p$, with $p(m)\ge m+2$, such that a word $w$ of length $m$ can be transformed into a product scalar-image instance at $r=2(p(m)+2)\ge8$ that is consistent exactly when $w\in L_0$. It has $O_M((r+1)^2+m)$ real types and comparison schemas, with dimensions in $\{1,2\}$, and is generated in deterministic polynomial time in $m$.
  - Explanation: Choosing a complement decider aligns scalar-image consistency with membership in the source language.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-fibers)): For every block $B$, $\mathcal Z_B^0$ is a vector space and $\mathcal Z_B^1$ is a nonempty affine translate of it. More precisely, $$\mathcal Z_B^0+\xi=\mathcal Z_B^1\qquad\text{for every }\xi\in\mathcal Z_B^1.$$ Projection of either fiber to any one readout name is onto that readout’s entire value space. Every valuation uses at most six bits.
  - Explanation: Legal local fibers are nonempty affine translates, with full projection onto each individual readout.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-exact-projection)): If the product scalar-image instance is consistent, there are nonempty families $\mathcal H_U$, for every $U\subseteq\mathcal B$ with $\abs{U}\le K$, of lists $$\boldsymbol\xi=(\xi_B)_{B\in U},\qquad \xi_B\in\mathcal Z_B^1,$$ such that the offsets within a list agree on every shared readout and $$\mathcal H_U\|_V=\mathcal H_V \qquad\text{whenever }V\subseteq U. \label{eq:exact-projection}$$ Here restriction deletes the entries belonging to $U\setminus V$.
  - Explanation: Consistency supplies compatible offset families with exact restriction, so every selected smaller list extends.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-offset-lem-support)): If $\abs{U}\le K$, the support of every component of $\Gamma_U$ is a tree with at most one real type. Without a real type, it lies in the interior of a single wire. With a real type, it consists of that type and possibly initial arms of wires incident with it.
  - Explanation: Small block collections cannot traverse a whole subdivided wire and therefore have tree supports with at most one real type.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-offset-lem-wrapping)): Let $\abs{U}\le r+1$.* 1. *Every wrapping subcollection of $U$ has at least $r$ blocks.* 2. *A wrapping subcollection with exactly $r$ blocks has one block in each row, lies in a single corner component, and determines a unique full aligned address.* 3. *Any two wrapping $r$-block subcollections of $U$ lie in the same corner component and determine the same address in that component’s alignment.* 4. *If the support of a wrapping $r$-block subcollection has no real type, it lies in a single wire $e$, and its recovered address, expressed in that wire’s internal labels, belongs to $A_e$.
  - Explanation: Wrapping requires all coordinate rows; a minimal wrapping collection recovers a unique aligned address.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-offset-lem-shifts-exist)): Every set $\mathcal S(C)$ just defined is nonempty.
  - Explanation: Shift systems exist by choosing a real-type vector or an internal scalar and propagating along tree arms.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-offset-lem-wrapping-lift)): Let $V\subsetneq U\subseteq\mathcal B$, with $\abs{U}\le r+1$. If $C_0$ is a wrapping component of $\Gamma_V$, and $C$ is its containing component in $\Gamma_U$, restriction of shift systems defines a surjection $$\mathcal S(C)\longrightarrow\mathcal S(C_0).$$
  - Explanation: The individual scalar-image equality supplies the preimage needed to extend shifts from a wrapping component.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-offset-lem-gauge)): Let $C_0$ be a nonwrapping corner component. Suppose two shift systems $(h_t)$ and $(\widetilde h_t)$ satisfy the link equations on its support. Every collection of offsets represented by the first system and corner potentials can also be represented by the second system after changing those potentials.
  - Explanation: Changing corner potentials absorbs a shift change on a nonwrapping component while preserving all retained readouts.
- **C18** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-general-realization)): For the affine block data at address length $r\ge3$, the graphs $G^0,G^1$ are simple, uncolored, and of the same positive order. They have $O(J^3)$ vertices.* *Every $\xi_B\in\mathcal Z_B^1$ determines a fixed bijection $\Psi_{B,\xi_B}:V_B^0\to V_B^1$, depending only on $B,\xi_B$, by $$(B,z)\longmapsto(B,z+\xi_B),\qquad \operatorname{leaf}(B,z,\nu)\longmapsto \operatorname{leaf}(B,z+\xi_B,\nu).$$ For any set $U$ of blocks, offsets agreeing on all shared readouts make the union of these maps an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.* *At every history reached by a winning $K=r+1$-slot strategy, a marked base vertex $(B,z)$ is answered by $(B,z')$ with $z'\in\mathcal Z_B^1$. If zero base vertices of distinct blocks are simultaneously marked, their responses agree on every shared readout, even when all $K$ slots are occupied.
  - Explanation: Valuation vertices and private leaves realize block translations and permit recognition of zero-query responses.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-item-game-zero-decoding)): Let a product scalar-image instance have address length $r\ge3$, put $K=r+1$, and form its affine block data. Suppose finite simple graphs $W^0,W^1$ of the same positive order have partitions $$V(W^\varepsilon)=\coprod_{B\in\mathcal B}V_B^\varepsilon \qquad(\varepsilon\in\{0,1\})$$ with the following properties.* 1. * For every block $B$ and offset $\xi_B\in\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $\Psi_{B,\xi_B}:V_B^0\to V_B^1$, depending only on that offset and the constructed graphs. Whenever offsets on a set of blocks agree on shared readouts, the union of their maps is an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.* 2. *For every $B$, $\varepsilon$, and $z\in\mathcal Z_B^\varepsilon$, there is a nonempty set $\mathcal D_B^\varepsilon(z)\subseteq V_B^\varepsilon$; for fixed $B,\varepsilon$, these sets are pairwise disjoint. At every history reached under any winning $K$-slot strategy, a marked vertex in $\mathcal D_B^0(0)$ is answered by a vertex in $\mathcal D_B^1(\zeta_B)$ for some $\zeta_B\in\mathcal Z_B^1$. For simultaneous such marks in distinct blocks $B,C$, the decoded valuations $\zeta_B,\zeta_C$ agree on every shared readout. These assertions apply also when all slots are occupied.* *Then $$\text{the instance is consistent} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \text{Duplicator wins on \(W^0,W^1\) with \(K\) slots} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad W^0\equiv_r W^1.$$
  - Explanation: Local block bijections and zero-query decoding suffice to equate consistency with a winning announced-bijection strategy.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-general-game)): For every product scalar-image instance at address length $r\ge3$, its general realization satisfies $$\text{the instance is consistent}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad G^0\equiv_r G^1.$$
  - Explanation: The general realization meets the interface and transfers consistency to refinement equivalence.
- **C21** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-subcubic-realization)): From the block data one can construct graphs $X^0,X^1$, with partitions $$V(X^\varepsilon)=\coprod_{B\in\mathcal B}V_B^\varepsilon \qquad(\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}),$$ having the following properties.* 1. *Both graphs are finite, simple, connected, uncolored, and of maximum degree at most three. They have the same positive order. Their orders are $O(N^4)$, and the construction is polynomial in the size of the explicitly listed block data.* 2. *For each $z\in\mathcal Z_B^\varepsilon$, the part $V_B^\varepsilon$ contains a specified triangle $T_B^\varepsilon(z)$. These triangles are pairwise disjoint. For every $\xi_B\in\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $$\Psi_{B,\xi_B}:V_B^0\longrightarrow V_B^1$$ depending only on $B,\xi_B$, and the constructed graphs. It sends $T_B^0(z)$ onto $T_B^1(z+\xi_B)$.* *If $U\subseteq\mathcal B$ and the offsets $(\xi_B)_{B\in U}$ agree on all names shared by blocks in $U$, then the following map is an isomorphism of induced subgraphs: $$\coprod_{B\in U}\Psi_{B,\xi_B}: X^0\left[\bigcup_{B\in U}V_B^0\right] \longrightarrow X^1\left[\bigcup_{B\in U}V_B^1\right].$$* 3. *Fix a winning Duplicator strategy in the $K$-slot bijection game, where $K=r+1\ge9$. At every reached history, a marked vertex in $T_B^0(z)$ is paired with a vertex in $T_B^1(z')$ for a unique $z'\in\mathcal Z_B^1$. If simultaneous marked vertices lie in $T_B^0(z)$ and $T_C^0(w)$, where $B\ne C$, then, for each $\rho\in\mathcal R(B)\cap\mathcal R(C)$, $$z\|_\rho=w\|_\rho \quad\Longrightarrow\quad z'\|_\rho=w'\|_\rho .$$ These conclusions hold even when all $K$ slots are occupied.
  - Explanation: Valuation spines, prefix forests and uncoloring gadgets realize the same interface with connected subcubic graphs.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-graph-colored-promises)): Each $Y^\varepsilon$ is simple, connected, and has maximum degree at most three. Each block part is connected. There are $O(N^2)$ vertices and $O(N^2)$ sorts.
  - Explanation: The colored intermediate graph has connected block parts and bounded degree with quadratic vertex and sort counts.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-graph-readout-walk)): For distinct blocks $B,C$ and a shared name $\rho$, there is a fixed sequence of sorts such that $s_B^\varepsilon(z)$ and $s_C^\varepsilon(w)$ are joined by a walk following that sequence if and only if $z\|_\rho=w\|_\rho$. The sequence is common to both versions.
  - Explanation: A prescribed sort sequence tests equality of a shared readout through prefix roots and their equality matching.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-graph-colored-translations)): Every $\xi_B\in\mathcal Z_B^1$ determines a color-preserving isomorphism between the block parts of $B$ in $Y^0,Y^1$. It fixes the anchor, sends $$s_{Bc}^0(z)\longmapsto s_{Bc}^1(z+\xi_B), \qquad f_{Bc,h}^0(u)\longmapsto f_{Bc,h}^1\bigl(u+\pi_{Bc,h}(\xi_B)\bigr),$$ and depends on no other block’s offset. On a union of parts, these maps form an induced-subgraph isomorphism whenever the chosen offsets agree on shared names.
  - Explanation: A legal block offset translates valuations and prefixes while preserving compatible matching edges.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-graph-uncolored-lifts)): The graphs $X^\varepsilon$ are simple, connected, uncolored, and of maximum degree at most three. They have the same positive order, of size $O(N^4)$. Each colored block translation in [graph:colored-translations](#graph:colored-translations) has a fixed lift $\Psi_{B,\xi_B}:V_B^0\to V_B^1$, depending only on that offset, satisfying the induced-isomorphism assertion of [prop:subcubic-realization](#prop:subcubic-realization).
  - Explanation: Triangle, connector and private-path replacement removes colors and lifts block translations locally.
- **C26** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-recognition)): Let $K\ge6$, and fix a winning strategy on $X^0,X^1$. At every reached history:* 1. *Paired vertices have the same degree and agree as to whether they lie in a triangle.* 2. *A triangle member over $v\in V(Y^0)$ is paired with a triangle member over some $v'\in V(Y^1)$ of the same sort.* 3. *If two marked triangle members lie over adjacent vertices $v,w$ of $Y^0$, their responses lie over adjacent vertices $v',w'$ of $Y^1$.* 4. *A marked member of $T_B^0(z)$ has a unique decoded response $z'\in\mathcal Z_B^1$, meaning that its response belongs to $T_B^1(z')$. For simultaneous marked members at distinct blocks, equality of any shared readout implies equality of the decoded response readouts.* *The conclusions do not require a free slot at the history in question.
  - Explanation: Hypothetical continuations force degree, sort, underlying adjacency and shared-readout invariants at reached histories.
- **C27** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-subcubic-game)): For every product scalar-image instance at address length $r\ge8$, its connected subcubic realization satisfies $$\text{the instance is consistent}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad X^0\equiv_r X^1.$$
  - Explanation: The connected subcubic realization transfers consistency to refinement equivalence.
- **C28** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-general)): Fix the machine $M$ and polynomial $p$ used in Proposition <a href="#prop:computation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:computation">7</a>. For a word $w$ of length $m$, put $r=2(p(m)+2)$. The constructions of [prop:computation](#prop:computation), [prop:consistency](#prop:consistency), [prop:general-realization](#prop:general-realization) produce two equally large simple uncolored graphs of order $O_M((r+1)^{12})$. Their explicit adjacency matrices and the binary parameter $k=r$ can be produced by a deterministic multitape Turing machine in time $O_{M,p}((r+2)^{80})$.
  - Explanation: Sequential enumeration produces explicit general-graph adjacency matrices with uniform polynomial bounds.
- **C29** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-construction)): Fix a machine $M$ and polynomial $p$ satisfying [prop:computation](#prop:computation), and apply the buffer construction of [prop:consistency](#prop:consistency). On an input word of length $m$, put $$r=2(p(m)+2),\qquad K=r+1,\qquad b_*=r+2.$$ The two graphs $X^0,X^1$ and the binary dimension $k=r$ can be produced deterministically in time $O_{M,p}(b_*^{160})$. Each output graph has $O_M(b_*^{16})$ vertices. They have the same positive order and are connected, simple, uncolored, undirected graphs of maximum degree at most three, regardless of whether the scalar-image instance is consistent.
  - Explanation: Sequential enumeration produces explicit connected subcubic adjacency matrices without enumerating witnesses or full addresses.
- **C30** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-unary-dimension)): If the supplied dimension is encoded in unary instead of binary, both $\textnormal{\textsc{WL-Equiv}}$ and $\textnormal{\textsc{Subcubic-WL-Equiv}}$ remain $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$-complete under deterministic polynomial-time many-one reductions.
  - Explanation: The completeness reductions still run in polynomial time when their output dimension is written in unary.

        "exact_quote": "For every binary language $L_0\\in\\mathsf{EXPTIME}$, there are a fixed machine $M$ and fixed integer polynomial $p$, with $p(m)\\ge m+2$, such that a word $w$ of length $m$ can be transformed into a product scalar-image instance at $r=2(p(m)+2)\\ge8$ that is consistent exactly when $w\\in L_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exact-projection",
        "exact_quote": "If the product scalar-image instance is consistent, there are nonempty families $\\mathcal H_U$, for every $U\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ with $\\abs{U}\\le K$, of lists $$\\boldsymbol\\xi=(\\xi_B)_{B\\in U},\\qquad \\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1,$$ such that the offsets within a list agree on every shared readout and $$\\mathcal H_U|_V=\\mathcal H_V\n       \\qquad\\text{whenever }V\\subseteq U.\n       \\label{eq:exact-projection}$$ Here restriction deletes the entries belonging to $U\\setminus V$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-offset-lem-wrapping",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\abs{U}\\le r+1$.*\n\n1.  *Every wrapping subcollection of $U$ has at least $r$ blocks.*\n\n2.  *A wrapping subcollection with exactly $r$ blocks has one block in each row, lies in a single corner component, and determines a unique full aligned address.*\n\n3.  *Any two wrapping $r$-block subcollections of $U$ lie in the same corner component and determine the same address in that component’s alignment.*\n\n4.  *If the support of a wrapping $r$-block subcollection has no real type, it lies in a single wire $e$, and its recovered address, expressed in that wire’s internal labels, belongs to $A_e$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-general-realization",
        "exact_quote": "For the affine block data at address length $r\\ge3$, the graphs $G^0,G^1$ are simple, uncolored, and of the same positive order. They have $O(J^3)$ vertices.*\n\n*Every $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$ determines a fixed bijection $\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\to V_B^1$, depending only on $B,\\xi_B$, by $$(B,z)\\longmapsto(B,z+\\xi_B),\\qquad\n \\operatorname{leaf}(B,z,\\nu)\\longmapsto\n \\operatorname{leaf}(B,z+\\xi_B,\\nu).$$ For any set $U$ of blocks, offsets agreeing on all shared readouts make the union of these maps an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.*\n\n*At every history reached by a winning $K=r+1$-slot strategy, a marked base vertex $(B,z)$ is answered by $(B,z')$ with $z'\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. If zero base vertices of distinct blocks are simultaneously marked, their responses agree on every shared readout, even when all $K$ slots are occupied.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-133.4"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-item-game-zero-decoding",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:game-reduction\" label=\"prop:game-reduction\"></span> Let a product scalar-image instance have address length $r\\ge3$, put $K=r+1$, and form its affine block data. Suppose finite simple graphs $W^0,W^1$ of the same positive order have partitions $$V(W^\\varepsilon)=\\coprod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}V_B^\\varepsilon\n \\qquad(\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\})$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:game-local-maps\" label=\"item:game-local-maps\"></span> For every block $B$ and offset $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\to V_B^1$, depending only on that offset and the constructed graphs. Whenever offsets on a set of blocks agree on shared readouts, the union of their maps is an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.*\n\n2.  *For every $B$, $\\varepsilon$, and $z\\in\\mathcal Z_B^\\varepsilon$, there is a nonempty set $\\mathcal D_B^\\varepsilon(z)\\subseteq V_B^\\varepsilon$; for fixed $B,\\varepsilon$, these sets are pairwise disjoint. At every history reached under any winning $K$-slot strategy, a marked vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^0(0)$ is answered by a vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^1(\\zeta_B)$ for some $\\zeta_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. For simultaneous such marks in distinct blocks $B,C$, the decoded valuations $\\zeta_B,\\zeta_C$ agree on every shared readout. These assertions apply also when all slots are occupied.*\n\n*Then $$\\text{the instance is consistent}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\text{Duplicator wins on \\(W^0,W^1\\) with \\(K\\) slots}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad W^0\\equiv_r W^1.$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-133.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-item-game-zero-decoding",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:game-reduction\" label=\"prop:game-reduction\"></span> Let a product scalar-image instance have address length $r\\ge3$, put $K=r+1$, and form its affine block data. Suppose finite simple graphs $W^0,W^1$ of the same positive order have partitions $$V(W^\\varepsilon)=\\coprod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}V_B^\\varepsilon\n \\qquad(\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\})$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:game-local-maps\" label=\"item:game-local-maps\"></span> For every block $B$ and offset $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\to V_B^1$, depending only on that offset and the constructed graphs. Whenever offsets on a set of blocks agree on shared readouts, the union of their maps is an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.*\n\n2.  *For every $B$, $\\varepsilon$, and $z\\in\\mathcal Z_B^\\varepsilon$, there is a nonempty set $\\mathcal D_B^\\varepsilon(z)\\subseteq V_B^\\varepsilon$; for fixed $B,\\varepsilon$, these sets are pairwise disjoint. At every history reached under any winning $K$-slot strategy, a marked vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^0(0)$ is answered by a vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^1(\\zeta_B)$ for some $\\zeta_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. For simultaneous such marks in distinct blocks $B,C$, the decoded valuations $\\zeta_B,\\zeta_C$ agree on every shared readout. These assertions apply also when all slots are occupied.*\n\n*Then $$\\text{the instance is consistent}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\text{Duplicator wins on \\(W^0,W^1\\) with \\(K\\) slots}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad W^0\\equiv_r W^1.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-133.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"prop:game-reduction\" label=\"prop:game-reduction\"></span> Let a product scalar-image instance have address length $r\\ge3$, put $K=r+1$, and form its affine block data. Suppose finite simple graphs $W^0,W^1$ of the same positive order have partitions $$V(W^\\varepsilon)=\\coprod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}V_B^\\varepsilon\n \\qquad(\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\})$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:game-local-maps\" label=\"item:game-local-maps\"></span> For every block $B$ and offset $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\to V_B^1$, depending only on that offset and the constructed graphs. Whenever offsets on a set of blocks agree on shared readouts, the union of their maps is an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.*\n\n2.  *For every $B$, $\\varepsilon$, and $z\\in\\mathcal Z_B^\\varepsilon$, there is a nonempty set $\\mathcal D_B^\\varepsilon(z)\\subseteq V_B^\\varepsilon$; for fixed $B,\\varepsilon$, these sets are pairwise disjoint. At every history reached under any winning $K$-slot strategy, a marked vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^0(0)$ is answered by a vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^1(\\zeta_B)$ for some $\\zeta_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. For simultaneous such marks in distinct blocks $B,C$, the decoded valuations $\\zeta_B,\\zeta_C$ agree on every shared readout. These assertions apply also when all slots are occupied.*\n\n*Then $$\\text{the instance is consistent}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\text{Duplicator wins on \\(W^0,W^1\\) with \\(K\\) slots}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad W^0\\equiv_r W^1.$$",
      "explanation": "Local block bijections and zero-query decoding suffice to equate consistency with a winning announced-bijection strategy.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-subcubic-realization",
        "exact_quote": "From the block data one can construct graphs $X^0,X^1$, with partitions $$V(X^\\varepsilon)=\\coprod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}V_B^\\varepsilon\n  \\qquad(\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}),$$ having the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Both graphs are finite, simple, connected, uncolored, and of maximum degree at most three. They have the same positive order. Their orders are $O(N^4)$, and the construction is polynomial in the size of the explicitly listed block data.*\n\n2.  *For each $z\\in\\mathcal Z_B^\\varepsilon$, the part $V_B^\\varepsilon$ contains a specified triangle $T_B^\\varepsilon(z)$. These triangles are pairwise disjoint. For every $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $$\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\longrightarrow V_B^1$$ depending only on $B,\\xi_B$, and the constructed graphs. It sends $T_B^0(z)$ onto $T_B^1(z+\\xi_B)$.*\n\n    *If $U\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ and the offsets $(\\xi_B)_{B\\in U}$ agree on all names shared by blocks in $U$, then the following map is an isomorphism of induced subgraphs: $$\\coprod_{B\\in U}\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:\n     X^0\\left[\\bigcup_{B\\in U}V_B^0\\right]\n     \\longrightarrow\n     X^1\\left[\\bigcup_{B\\in U}V_B^1\\right].$$*\n\n3.  *Fix a winning Duplicator strategy in the $K$-slot bijection game, where $K=r+1\\ge9$. At every reached history, a marked vertex in $T_B^0(z)$ is paired with a vertex in $T_B^1(z')$ for a unique $z'\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. If simultaneous marked vertices lie in $T_B^0(z)$ and $T_C^0(w)$, where $B\\ne C$, then, for each $\\rho\\in\\mathcal R(B)\\cap\\mathcal R(C)$, $$z|_\\rho=w|_\\rho\n         \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n         z'|_\\rho=w'|_\\rho .$$ These conclusions hold even when all $K$ slots are occupied.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-133.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $K\\ge6$, and fix a winning strategy on $X^0,X^1$.         "exact_quote": "Fix the machine $M$ and polynomial $p$ used in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:computation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:computation\">7</a>.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a machine $M$ and polynomial $p$ satisfying [prop:computation](#prop:computation), and apply the buffer construction of [prop:consistency](#prop:consistency). On an input word of length $m$, put $$r=2(p(m)+2),\\qquad K=r+1,\\qquad b_*=r+2.$$ The two graphs $X^0,X^1$ and the binary dimension $k=r$ can be produced deterministically in time $O_{M,p}(b_*^{160})$. Each output graph has $O_M(b_*^{16})$ vertices. They have the same positive order and are connected, simple, uncolored, undirected graphs of maximum degree at most three, regardless of whether the scalar-image instance is consistent.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-133.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a machine $M$ and polynomial $p$ satisfying [prop:computation](#prop:computation), and apply the buffer construction of [prop:consistency](#prop:consistency). Each output graph has $O_M(b_*^{16})$ vertices. They have the same positive order and are connected, simple, uncolored, undirected graphs of maximum degree at most three, regardless of whether the scalar-image instance is consistent.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Joint-update Weisfeiler–Leman refinement** ([source](#stmt-def-joint-wl)): Let $X$ be a finite simple graph and let $k\geq 2$. For $\mathbf v=(v_1,\ldots,v_k)\in V(X)^k$, write $\mathbf v[i\leftarrow z]$ for the tuple obtained by replacing its $i$th coordinate by $z$. The initial color $\chi_0^X(\mathbf v)$ records, for every ordered pair of coordinates, whether their entries are equal and whether they are adjacent. Recursively, set $$\label{game:joint-update} \chi_{s+1}^X(\mathbf v)= \left(\chi_s^X(\mathbf v), \left\{\!\!\left\{ \bigl(\chi_s^X(\mathbf v[1\leftarrow z]),\ldots, \chi_s^X(\mathbf v[k\leftarrow z])\bigr):z\in V(X) \right\}\!\!\right\}\right).$$ The braces in the second component denote a multiset. The recursive names of colors are common to all graphs under comparison. We write $G\equiv_k H$ if, at every round $s\geq 0$, the multisets of colors on $V(G)^k$ and $V(H)^k$ agree.
- **Product description** ([source](#stmt-def-computation-product)): For an address length $r$, every gate type has one gate at each address in $\{0,1\}^r$. A connection schema specifies source and destination types, a destination port, nonempty coordinate domains $A_i\subseteq\{0,1\}$, and injections $\sigma_i:A_i\longrightarrow\{0,1\}$, for $1\le i\le r$. It describes the connections $$(\text{source type},\mathbf a) \longrightarrow (\text{destination type},\boldsymbol\sigma(\mathbf a)), \qquad \mathbf a\in\prod_{i=1}^r A_i,$$ at the specified port, where $\boldsymbol\sigma(\mathbf a)=(\sigma_i(a_i))_{i=1}^r$. A seed or test request specifies a scalar type and a product domain of addresses. The description lists these schemas and requests by their one-coordinate tables, rather than listing all addressed gates.
- **Product consistency instance** ([source](#stmt-def-scalar-images)): Fix an address length $r$. A finite nonempty set $\mathcal P$ of *real types* has dimensions $d_P\in\{1,2\}$. For every $P\in\mathcal P$ and every $\mathbf a\in\{0,1\}^r$, there is a variable with value space $\mathbb F_2^{d_P}$. A schema $e\in\mathcal E$ consists of ordered endpoint types $P,R$, a product domain $$A_e=\prod_{i=1}^r A_{e,i},\qquad \varnothing\ne A_{e,i}\subseteq\{0,1\},$$ coordinate injections $\sigma_{e,i}:A_{e,i}\to\{0,1\}$, and scalar affine forms $\phi_{e,0}$ on $\mathbb F_2^{d_P}$ and $\phi_{e,1}$ on $\mathbb F_2^{d_R}$. Write $\boldsymbol\sigma_e$ for their componentwise address map. Endpoint types may coincide, parallel schemas are allowed, and the linear part of an affine form may be zero. The instance is *consistent* if there are nonempty sets $Q_{P,\mathbf a}\subseteq\mathbb F_2^{d_P}$ for all variables such that $$\label{eq:scalar-images} \phi_{e,0}(Q_{P,\mathbf a}) =\phi_{e,1}(Q_{R,\boldsymbol\sigma_e(\mathbf a)}) \qquad(e\in\mathcal E,\ \mathbf a\in A_e).$$ Here $\phi(Q)=\{\phi(u):u\in Q\}$. Each equality compares one scalar image at each end; several comparisons are never combined into a joint image condition.
- **Initial tuple color** ([source](#stmt-def-joint-wl)): The initial color $\chi_0^X(\mathbf v)$ records, for every ordered pair of coordinates, whether their entries are equal and whether they are adjacent.
- **Refinement equivalence** ([source](#stmt-def-joint-wl)): We write $G\equiv_k H$ if, at every round $s\geq 0$, the multisets of colors on $V(G)^k$ and $V(H)^k$ agree.
- **Scalar image** ([source](#stmt-def-scalar-images)): Here $\phi(Q)=\{\phi(u):u\in Q\}$.
- **Corner graph** ([source](#offset:small-supports)): For $U\subseteq\mathcal B$, let $\Gamma_U$ be the union of the corner diagrams of its blocks.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-cap** [claim](#stmt-lem-dimension-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dimension-cap): Use a tuple containing every vertex to recover an isomorphism. -> A safe cap before tuple-table allocation.
- **R-upper** [claim](#stmt-prop-membership) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-membership): Validate encodings, cap dimension and refine common tuple signatures to stabilization. -> Uniform exponential-time membership.
- **R-game** [claim](#stmt-lem-wl-game) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wl-game): Match replacement vectors by announced bijections and recover histogram equality by inverting the strategy tree. -> The empty-position game characterization.
- **R-circuit** [claim](#stmt-prop-computation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-computation): Compile carry, borrow, initial and terminal product domains. -> An addressed acyclic circuit.
- **R-images** [claim](#stmt-prop-consistency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-consistency): Introduce private buffers and compare individual scalar images. -> Consistency exactly when every test is false.
- **R-project** [claim](#stmt-prop-exact-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-offset-lem-gauge-2): Use admissible shifts, wrapping preimages and nonwrapping corner-potential changes. -> Compatible families with exact restriction.
- **R-general** [claim](#stmt-prop-general-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-general-realization): Enumerate local valuations and attach block-distinguishing private leaves. -> General graphs with local maps and zero decoding.
- **R-transfer** [claim](#stmt-item-game-zero-decoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-item-game-zero-decoding): Extend each inactive block separately before announcing a bijection; recover witness sets by attained ring sums. -> Consistency exactly when the game is won.
- **R-subcubic** [claim](#stmt-prop-subcubic-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-recognition-2): Build valuation spines and prefix forests, then remove sorts with triangle and path gadgets. -> Connected subcubic graphs meeting the realization interface.
- **R-general-compiler** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-general) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-general): Enumerate compressed descriptions and stream explicit matrix entries using sequential lists. -> Uniform polynomial general compiler.
- **R-subcubic-compiler** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-construction): Enumerate block pairs, local prefixes, ports and output vertex pairs. -> Uniform polynomial subcubic compiler.
- **R-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-construction-2): Use a complement decider and combine the compiler, consistency and game equivalences. -> Completeness under deterministic polynomial-time many-one reductions.

All domains have nonempty binary factors and all coordinate maps are injections. The total number of coordinate entries is $O_M(r((r+1)^2+m))$.           "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:game-reduction\" label=\"prop:game-reduction\"></span> Let a product scalar-image instance have address length $r\\ge3$, put $K=r+1$, and form its affine block data. Suppose finite simple graphs $W^0,W^1$ of the same positive order have partitions $$V(W^\\varepsilon)=\\coprod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}V_B^\\varepsilon\n \\qquad(\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\})$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"item:game-local-maps\" label=\"item:game-local-maps\"></span> For every block $B$ and offset $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\to V_B^1$, depending only on that offset and the constructed graphs. Whenever offsets on a set of blocks agree on shared readouts, the union of their maps is an isomorphism of the induced subgraphs on the corresponding parts.*\n\n2.  *For every $B$, $\\varepsilon$, and $z\\in\\mathcal Z_B^\\varepsilon$, there is a nonempty set $\\mathcal D_B^\\varepsilon(z)\\subseteq V_B^\\varepsilon$; for fixed $B,\\varepsilon$, these sets are pairwise disjoint. At every history reached under any winning $K$-slot strategy, a marked vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^0(0)$ is answered by a vertex in $\\mathcal D_B^1(\\zeta_B)$ for some $\\zeta_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. For simultaneous such marks in distinct blocks $B,C$, the decoded valuations $\\zeta_B,\\zeta_C$ agree on every shared readout. These assertions apply also when all slots are occupied.*\n\n*Then $$\\text{the instance is consistent}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\text{Duplicator wins on \\(W^0,W^1\\) with \\(K\\) slots}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad W^0\\equiv_r W^1.$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-subcubic-realization",
          "exact_quote": "From the block data one can construct graphs $X^0,X^1$, with partitions $$V(X^\\varepsilon)=\\coprod_{B\\in\\mathcal B}V_B^\\varepsilon\n  \\qquad(\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}),$$ having the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Both graphs are finite, simple, connected, uncolored, and of maximum degree at most three. They have the same positive order. Their orders are $O(N^4)$, and the construction is polynomial in the size of the explicitly listed block data.*\n\n2.  *For each $z\\in\\mathcal Z_B^\\varepsilon$, the part $V_B^\\varepsilon$ contains a specified triangle $T_B^\\varepsilon(z)$. These triangles are pairwise disjoint. For every $\\xi_B\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$, there is a fixed bijection $$\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:V_B^0\\longrightarrow V_B^1$$ depending only on $B,\\xi_B$, and the constructed graphs. It sends $T_B^0(z)$ onto $T_B^1(z+\\xi_B)$.*\n\n    *If $U\\subseteq\\mathcal B$ and the offsets $(\\xi_B)_{B\\in U}$ agree on all names shared by blocks in $U$, then the following map is an isomorphism of induced subgraphs: $$\\coprod_{B\\in U}\\Psi_{B,\\xi_B}:\n     X^0\\left[\\bigcup_{B\\in U}V_B^0\\right]\n     \\longrightarrow\n     X^1\\left[\\bigcup_{B\\in U}V_B^1\\right].$$*\n\n3.  *Fix a winning Duplicator strategy in the $K$-slot bijection game, where $K=r+1\\ge9$. At every reached history, a marked vertex in $T_B^0(z)$ is paired with a vertex in $T_B^1(z')$ for a unique $z'\\in\\mathcal Z_B^1$. If simultaneous marked vertices lie in $T_B^0(z)$ and $T_C^0(w)$, where $B\\ne C$, then, for each $\\rho\\in\\mathcal R(B)\\cap\\mathcal R(C)$, $$z|_\\rho=w|_\\rho\n         \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n         z'|_\\rho=w'|_\\rho .$$ These conclusions hold even when all $K$ slots are occupied.",

The constructions of [prop:computation](#prop:computation), [prop:consistency](#prop:consistency), [prop:general-realization](#prop:general-realization) produce two equally large simple uncolored graphs of order $O_M((r+1)^{12})$. Their explicit adjacency matrices and the binary parameter $k=r$ can be produced by a deterministic multitape Turing machine in time $O_{M,p}((r+2)^{80})$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a machine $M$ and polynomial $p$ satisfying [prop:computation](#prop:computation), and apply the buffer construction of [prop:consistency](#prop:consistency). On an input word of length $m$, put $$r=2(p(m)+2),\\qquad K=r+1,\\qquad b_*=r+2.$$ The two graphs $X^0,X^1$ and the binary dimension $k=r$ can be produced deterministically in time $O_{M,p}(b_*^{160})$. Each output graph has $O_M(b_*^{16})$ vertices. They have the same positive order and are connected, simple, uncolored, undirected graphs of maximum degree at most three, regardless of whether the scalar-image instance is consistent.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-133.4"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The private buffer compares each coordinate image separately with the source output image and its sum image with the destination input image. A zero source image forces the zero buffer; a full source image supports every nonempty destination image.
- **I2**: Refinement uses common color names and keeps coordinate replacements for the same vertex grouped together.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Replace individual image comparisons by a simultaneous vector assignment. -> Different comparisons may require different supporting vectors.
- **A2**: Treat bounded-degree graph isomorphism hardness as the result proved here. -> Refinement equivalence at a supplied dimension is a different query from graph isomorphism.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The private buffer has finite binary value space and separate scalar-image comparisons.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with nonempty scalar images O and D on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: O and D are lists of distinct integers from {0,1}.
# OUTPUTS: JSON sorted list of masks encoding all nonempty buffer sets.
# TIME: Constant, exhaustive enumeration of fifteen subsets of four vectors.
# SPACE: Constant, at most fifteen masks.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer bits; addition over the binary field is XOR.
# FAILURE: Invalid image lists raise ValueError and exit nonzero.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz
v = json.load(sys.stdin)
o, d = set(v['O']), set(v['D'])
if any(type(x) is not int for key in ('O','D') for x in v[key]) or any(not s or not s <= {0,1} or len(s) != len(v[key]) for s,key in ((o,'O'),(d,'D'))):
    raise ValueError('invalid scalar images')
answer = []
for mask in range(1, 16):
    points = [(i//2, i%2) for i in range(4) if (int(fmpz(mask)) >> i) & 1]
    left = {a for a,b in points}
    right = {b for a,b in points}
    sums = {a ^ b for a,b in points}
    if left == o and right == o and sums == d:
        answer.append(mask)
print(json.dumps(answer))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The equivalence and game statements use joint updates with common color names; coordinate replacements for a single vertex must remain grouped. Blocked use: ['Replacing the joint vector multiset with separate coordinate multisets.']
- **L02** (hypothesis): The decision languages use explicit adjacency matrices of finite simple uncolored undirected graphs, with the supplied dimension meeting the stated input conditions. Blocked use: ['Transferring the completeness result to succinct graph encodings or arbitrary directed, colored, or nonsimple inputs.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The restricted language requires connected graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three. Blocked use: ['Treating disconnected graphs, unequal orders, or higher degrees as valid inputs of the restricted language.']
- **L04** (edge_case): The dimension cap assumes equal positive graph orders, and its displayed minimum-dimension equivalence assumes graph order at least two. Blocked use: ['Applying the displayed cap without checking the small-order cases and dimension conditions.']
- **L05** (counterexample_scope): The degree restriction is an upper bound; the constructed graphs need not be regular. Blocked use: ['Promoting subcubic hardness to a hardness statement for regular graphs.']
- **L06** (counterexample_scope): Pair equivalence at a supplied dimension differs from identifying one graph among all possible mates and from bounded-degree graph isomorphism. Blocked use: ['Using this theorem as a theorem about identification dimension or bounded-degree graph-isomorphism hardness.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): Scalar-image consistency compares individual images of nonempty sets; it does not provide a simultaneous vector assignment. Blocked use: ['Replacing separate scalar-image equalities by a joint image condition or a globally satisfying assignment.']
- **L08** (nonconstructive): Witness sets, compatible offset families, and shifts certify existence in the proof and are not enumerated by the graph reduction. Blocked use: ['Claiming the graph compiler extracts consistency witnesses or offset families.']
- **L09** (hypothesis): The scalar-image circuit construction assumes a finite acyclic circuit with terminal tests and the stated gate conventions. Blocked use: ['Applying circuit consistency to cyclic circuits or allowing tests to serve as propagation sources.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The computation compiler fixes the machine and polynomial; uniformity concerns the growing address dimension while constants may depend on the fixed machine and polynomial. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the compiler bound as independent of an input machine description or replacing it with a fixed-dimension theorem.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): Duplicator announces a whole vertex bijection before Spoiler chooses a vertex. Exact projection permits preserving the selected old offsets, rather than merely finding some compatible list. Blocked use: ['Answering an already selected vertex instead of announcing a whole bijection; using mere nonemptiness in place of exact extension.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): The executable scalar-image buffer checks can establish the finite local buffer behavior. They do not certify the full uniform reduction, recognition proof, or completeness theorem. Blocked use: ['Promoting a local buffer certificate to verification of the whole paper.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 133 (`lean/docs/133.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Parity lifts and bounded-treewidth witnesses for Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence](../../preprints/Parity-lifts-and-bounded-treewidth-witnesses-for-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [The complexity of identifying a graph by Weisfeiler–Leman refinement](../../preprints/The-complexity-of-identifying-a-graph-by-Weisfeiler-Leman-refinement-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Unconditional time lower bounds for Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence](../../preprints/Unconditional-time-lower-bounds-for-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Variable-dimension Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence on general and subcubic graphs](../../preprints/Variable-dimension-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-on-general-and-subcubic-graphs-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives the paper's parity-lift graph construction for every Weisfeiler–Leman dimension $k\ge4$, under both joint and separate conventions. From a finite choice system it constructs two equal-size uncolored graphs that are Weisfeiler–Leman equivalent exactly when the system has no successful compatible choice. The same equivalence holds for the pure variant.
>
> A successful choice is detected by the specified early round: round two in the joint convention and round one in the separate convention. The graph-size bound is linear in the choice-system size with a factor depending on $k$. The paper's conditional running-time exclusions are outside these selected construction statements.
>
> The formalization proves that deciding whether Weisfeiler–Leman refinement of a supplied dimension identifies a graph is EXPTIME-complete under polynomial-time many-one reductions. The input is a nonempty finite simple uncolored graph encoded by its adjacency matrix together with a positive binary-encoded dimension. Identification quantifies over every comparison graph, and the statement includes both the exponential-time upper bound and hardness.
>
> The formalization proves an unconditional deterministic time lower bound for deciding $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence. For every sufficiently large fixed $k$, every deciding algorithm in the stated multitape Turing-machine or sequential logarithmic-word RAM model requires at least $n^{ck}$ worst-case time for all sufficiently large graph orders $n$, for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The bound covers both joint and separate replacement conventions and remains valid for explicit adjacency-matrix inputs restricted to simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two.
>
> The formalization proves EXPTIME-completeness of deciding joint-update $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence when $k\ge2$ is part of the input in binary and the graphs are encoded by explicit adjacency matrices. It proves both the general result and the restriction to connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three. Completeness uses polynomial-time many-one reductions.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace VariableWL

abbrev Word := List Bool

inductive Motion where
  | left | stay | right
  deriving DecidableEq

def Motion.displacement : Motion → ℤ
  | .left => -1
  | .stay => 0
  | .right => 1

structure Machine where
  states : ℕ
  workSymbols : ℕ
  extraTapes : ℕ
  transition : Fin (states + 1) →
    (Fin (extraTapes + 1) → Fin (workSymbols + 3)) →
    Option (Fin (states + 1) ×
      (Fin (extraTapes + 1) → Fin (workSymbols + 3) × Motion))

structure Configuration (M : Machine) where
  control : Fin (M.states + 1)
  heads : Fin (M.extraTapes + 1) → ℤ
  cells : Fin (M.extraTapes + 1) → ℤ → Fin (M.workSymbols + 3)

def wordCells (a : ℕ) (w : Word) (i : ℤ) : Fin (a + 3) :=
  if 0 ≤ i then
    match w[i.toNat]? with
    | none => 0
    | some false => 1
    | some true => 2
  else 0

def initial (M : Machine) (w : Word) : Configuration M where
  control := 0
  heads := fun _ => 0
  cells := fun j => if j = 0 then wordCells M.workSymbols w else fun _ => 0

def scanned {M : Machine} (c : Configuration M) :
    Fin (M.extraTapes + 1) → Fin (M.workSymbols + 3) :=
  fun j => c.cells j (c.heads j)

def step (M : Machine) (c : Configuration M) : Option (Configuration M) :=
  (M.transition c.control (scanned c)).map fun data =>
    { control := data.1
      heads := fun j => c.heads j + (data.2 j).2.displacement
      cells := fun j i => if i = c.heads j then (data.2 j).1 else c.cells j i }

def run (M : Machine) (w : Word) : ℕ → Configuration M
  | 0 => initial M w
  | n + 1 => (step M (run M w n)).getD (run M w n)

def OutputsWithin (M : Machine) (w out : Word) (bound : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ t ≤ bound, step M (run M w t) = none ∧
    (run M w t).cells 0 = wordCells M.workSymbols out

abbrev Language := Set Word

def InEXPTIME (L : Language) : Prop :=
  ∃ M : Machine, ∃ c d : ℕ, ∀ w : Word,
    ∃ b : Bool, (b = true ↔ w ∈ L) ∧
      OutputsWithin M w [b] (2 ^ (c * (w.length + 1) ^ d))

def PolytimeReduces (L R : Language) : Prop :=
  ∃ M : Machine, ∃ c d : ℕ, ∀ w : Word,
    ∃ out : Word, OutputsWithin M w out (c * (w.length + 1) ^ d) ∧
      (w ∈ L ↔ out ∈ R)

def EXPTIMEComplete (L : Language) : Prop :=
  InEXPTIME L ∧ ∀ R : Language, InEXPTIME R → PolytimeReduces R L

structure Graph where
  order : ℕ
  graph : SimpleGraph (Fin order)

abbrev Tuple (G : Graph) (k : ℕ) := Fin k → Fin G.order

def Color (k : ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Fin k → Fin k → Bool × Bool
  | s + 1 => Color k s × Multiset (Fin k → Color k s)

noncomputable def color (G : Graph) (k : ℕ) : (s : ℕ) → Tuple G k → Color k s
  | 0, v => by
      classical
      exact fun i j => (decide (v i = v j), decide (G.graph.Adj (v i) (v j)))
  | s + 1, v => (color G k s v,
      Finset.univ.val.map (fun z => fun i => color G k s (Function.update v i z)))

noncomputable def histogram (G : Graph) (k s : ℕ) : Multiset (Color k s) :=
  Finset.univ.val.map (color G k s)

def Equivalent (G H : Graph) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ s : ℕ, histogram G k s = histogram H k s

def integerCode (n : ℕ) : Word :=
  List.replicate (Nat.bits n).length true ++ [false] ++ Nat.bits n

noncomputable def graphCode (G : Graph) : Word := by
  classical
  exact integerCode G.order ++
    (List.ofFn fun i : Fin G.order =>
      List.ofFn fun j : Fin G.order => decide (G.graph.Adj i j)).flatten

noncomputable def pairCode (G H : Graph) (k : ℕ) : Word :=
  graphCode G ++ graphCode H ++ integerCode k

def WL : Language :=
  { w | ∃ G H : Graph, ∃ k : ℕ, w = pairCode G H k ∧ 2 ≤ k ∧ Equivalent G H k }

noncomputable def Subcubic (G : Graph) : Prop := by
  classical
  exact ∀ v : Fin G.order, G.graph.degree v ≤ 3

def SubWL : Language :=
  { w | ∃ G H : Graph, ∃ k : ℕ, w = pairCode G H k ∧ 2 ≤ k ∧
    0 < G.order ∧ G.order = H.order ∧ G.graph.Connected ∧ H.graph.Connected ∧
    Subcubic G ∧ Subcubic H ∧ Equivalent G H k }

theorem main : EXPTIMEComplete WL ∧ EXPTIMEComplete SubWL := by
  sorry

end VariableWL

end OAI
```

The same equivalence holds for the pure variant.\n\nA successful choice is detected by the specified early round: round two in the joint convention and round one in the separate convention. The graph-size bound is linear in the choice-system size with a factor depending on $k$. The paper's conditional running-time exclusions are outside these selected construction statements.\n\nThe formalization proves that deciding whether Weisfeiler–Leman refinement of a supplied dimension identifies a graph is EXPTIME-complete under polynomial-time many-one reductions. The input is a nonempty finite simple uncolored graph encoded by its adjacency matrix together with a positive binary-encoded dimension. Identification quantifies over every comparison graph, and the statement includes both the exponential-time upper bound and hardness.\n\nThe formalization proves an unconditional deterministic time lower bound for deciding $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence. For every sufficiently large fixed $k$, every deciding algorithm in the stated multitape Turing-machine or sequential logarithmic-word RAM model requires at least $n^{ck}$ worst-case time for all sufficiently large graph orders $n$, for one absolute $c>0$.\n\nThe bound covers both joint and separate replacement conventions and remains valid for explicit adjacency-matrix inputs restricted to simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two.\n\nThe formalization proves EXPTIME-completeness of deciding joint-update $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence when $k\\ge2$ is part of the input in binary and the graphs are encoded by explicit adjacency matrices. It proves both the general result and the restriction to connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three. Completeness uses polynomial-time many-one reductions."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Deciding joint-update <i>k</i>-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence is $`\mathsf{EXPTIME}`$-complete when the two graphs are given by explicit adjacency matrices and <i>k</i> ≥ 2 is encoded in binary. The result holds even for connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three.
